I mean, at the very beginning, before it all became so obvious, I tried to tell myself it was just normal bank problems or some mix-up with the loan servicer. But that didn’t last, because once the foreclosure notices started coming and the bank calls started taking over our home life, it was like everything changed.\n\nThat’s been going on for about a year and nine months now. Almost two years of it being in our house, in our conversations, in the mail, on the phone — just constantly there. And during that time, the way things lined up made it harder and harder for me to believe it was separate people doing separate things.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether there was some stretch in the middle where I thought, “No, they’re not connected,” I’d say no. Once it started with the foreclosure notices and those calls, I haven’t really been able to see it as anything else.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you ever changed or wavered since this all started?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say it has really wavered. I mean, I’ve gotten tired, and there have been moments where I’ve thought, maybe if I just look at the papers again, maybe there’s something I’m missing, because everyone keeps pushing that on me — like it’s just foreclosure paperwork and missed payments and normal procedure. But every time I try to look at it that way, it doesn’t fit with everything else I’ve seen.\n\nThat’s why I’m here, honestly. Because people keep acting like I’m supposed to accept whatever the bank or the loan servicer prints out and sends me, like that ends the discussion. But I know what this has been. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. It’s not one little misunderstanding. It’s been going on almost two years, and the way it all lines up is not something I can just unsee.\n\nSo yes, maybe I’ve questioned details here and there, like who knew what first or how they were communicating, but the main thing has not changed. No amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time, even for a brief moment, where you didn't believe the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job were working together against you?\n\nPatient: No. I mean, I understand why you’re asking it that way, like maybe there was some little window where I thought, “Oh, maybe this is all just ordinary paperwork.” But no, not really. Not once I saw how it was actually happening.\n\nPeople keep trying to reduce it to missed payments and foreclosure forms, like if the bank sends enough papers with logos and dates on them, then I’m supposed to just accept that as the whole truth. But that doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. That’s why I’m here — because everyone keeps acting like I’m the one not understanding it, when really they’re ignoring how it all lines up.\n\nMaybe there were moments where I was exhausted and wished I could believe it was just a mistake or normal procedure, because that would be easier. But believing it? No. No amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people couldn't hear?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I hear things everybody hears — the phone ringing, people talking outside, noises in the house, that kind of thing. Sometimes I’m on edge and I’ll notice sounds more, or I’ll think, “What was that?” and check, but I wouldn’t say I hear voices that other people can’t hear.\n\nIf you mean like a voice talking to me when nobody’s there, no. That’s not what this is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you are based on facts that most people would agree on?\n\nPatient: I think they’re based on facts, yes. I mean, maybe “most people” is where it gets complicated, because most people aren’t seeing the whole thing. They see one piece of paper from the bank, or one notice from the loan servicer, and they say, “Well, that’s just foreclosure paperwork,” or, “That’s what happens with missed payments.” But they’re not looking at how it all fits together, and they’re definitely not living inside it every day.\n\nThat’s part of why I’m here, honestly. Everyone keeps acting like if the bank prints something out, then that’s the final answer and I’m supposed to stop asking questions. But I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. It’s not just one form or one phone call or one misunderstanding. It’s been almost two years of things lining up in a way that doesn’t feel accidental.\n\nSo would most people agree? Maybe not right away, because they haven’t seen all of it the way I have. But that doesn’t mean it isn’t factual. No amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment where you completely stopped believing the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job were working together against you?\n\nPatient: No. I know it probably sounds repetitive, because I keep saying it, but no — I haven’t completely stopped believing it. I’ve had moments where I was tired, or scared, or I wished I could just accept what everyone else keeps saying, like, “It’s foreclosure paperwork, it’s missed payments, it’s just the normal process.” But that’s not the same as actually believing that.\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like this is just paperwork, like if the bank or the loan servicer sends enough notices then I’m supposed to ignore everything else. But I know what I’ve been seeing. It’s not just one letter or one phone call. It’s the way the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.\n\nSo, completely stopped believing it? No. I don’t think I have.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't think about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working against you for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: Several days in a row? No, not really. I mean, maybe there are a few hours where I’m busy at work or I’m trying to get through a shift and deal with customers and employees and all of that, and I’m not actively saying it in my head every second. But it’s still there. It doesn’t just disappear for days.\n\nBecause this isn’t some small thing I can put in a drawer. It’s my house. It’s been going on for almost two years, and everyone keeps trying to make it sound like it’s just foreclosure paperwork, missed payments, notices, normal bank procedure — but that’s not what I’m seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out. So even if I’m not thinking about it constantly every minute, something always brings it back. A letter, a call, something someone says at work, or just the thought of what they’re trying to do.\n\nSo no, I don’t think I’ve gone several days in a row without thinking about it. I wish I could, honestly, because it’s exhausting. But no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment of doubt about your beliefs regarding the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you?\n\nPatient: I mean… doubt in the way people want me to doubt it? No, not really. I’ve had moments where I get worn down, where I think, okay, maybe I should just stop fighting everyone and stop trying to explain it, because every time I bring it up people act like I’m making connections that aren’t there. But that’s not the same as doubting what I’ve seen.\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like it’s just forms and notices and I’m supposed to accept that explanation. But I know the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. That’s what keeps getting ignored. It isn’t one mistake or one late notice. It’s been going on for almost two years, and when I try to point out the pattern, people just show me more paperwork like that should settle it.\n\nSo yes, I’ve had moments where I wish I was wrong. I’ve had moments where I’m exhausted and I think, “Maybe it would be easier if this was just normal foreclosure stuff.” But then something else happens, or I think back over all of it, and it’s right there again. No amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that other people couldn't see?\n\nPatient: Seen things that other people couldn’t see? I don’t think so. I mean, not like… seeing a person standing there when nobody else does, or seeing things move that aren’t moving. That’s not really what this has been like for me.\n\nSometimes I notice things other people say they don’t notice, like the timing of things or the way certain things line up, but that’s different from seeing something with my eyes that nobody else can see. If you mean literally seeing things, like visions or objects or people that aren’t there, then no, I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nI may be more alert to things than other people, and maybe I pay attention to details they ignore, but I wouldn’t say I’m seeing things that aren’t visible.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about the conspiracy against you might be an exaggeration or not entirely based on reality?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s an exaggeration. I understand why you’re asking, because everybody keeps trying to frame it that way — like maybe I’m taking normal foreclosure notices and reading too much into them, or maybe I’m upset about missed payments and making it bigger than it is. But that’s not what this feels like from my side.\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like if they show me enough documents, then I’m supposed to say, “Oh, okay, I guess that explains everything.” But it doesn’t. I know the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. That sounds extreme when I say it out loud, I know it does, but I’ve been watching this for almost two years. It’s not one letter or one phone call or one misunderstanding.\n\nAnd no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing. If anything, the way they keep hiding behind paperwork makes me feel more like they know exactly what they’re doing. So no, I don’t think it’s not based in reality. I think other people don’t want to look at the whole pattern.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you might be incorrect?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it in the sense that people keep asking me to consider it. I mean, I’ve sat there and tried to say to myself, “Okay, what if this really is just the foreclosure process, what if it’s just missed payments and paperwork and nobody is coordinating anything?” I have tried to look at it that way because everyone acts like that’s the reasonable answer.\n\nBut when I go back over everything, it doesn’t hold. It doesn’t explain why it has felt so coordinated for almost two years. It doesn’t explain why the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job all seem to be moving in the same direction, like they’re quietly helping push me out of my house. People keep handing me documents like that’s supposed to erase the pattern, but paperwork doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing.\n\nSo yes, I’ve considered it because I’m being pressured to consider it, but I don’t believe I’m incorrect. I think other people are refusing to see what’s actually been happening.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes. I mean… yes, I do think certain things are related to me and what’s been happening with the house. Not every single thing, I’m not saying the whole world revolves around me or anything like that, but there are too many things in my daily life that line up with the foreclosure situation for me to just ignore them.

Like, people keep acting like this is just normal paperwork, missed payments, bank letters, legal notices — like I’m supposed to look at those papers and accept that there’s nothing else going on. But I’ve been watching this for almost two years now, and it feels connected. The bank, the loan servicer, even people at my job… it’s like they’re all moving in the same direction, quietly, carefully, to push me out of my house.

So when something happens during the day — a comment someone makes at work, the timing of a call, how certain paperwork shows up, how people suddenly get vague or avoid answering direct questions — yes, I notice it. And I do think it relates to my situation, because it fits the same pattern I’ve been seeing. That’s what people don’t understand. They look at each thing by itself and say, “That doesn’t prove anything.” But when you put it all together, it’s not random to me.

And no amount of documents they hand me changes that. If anything, the way everyone keeps insisting it’s just procedure makes me feel like they’re trying to keep me from seeing how connected it all really is.

I think other people are refusing to see what’s actually been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you think events or things you see in your daily life are specifically related to you or your situation?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… yes, I do think certain things are related to me and what’s been happening with the house. Not every single thing, I’m not saying the whole world revolves around me or anything like that, but there are too many things in my daily life that line up with the foreclosure situation for me to just ignore them.\n\nLike, people keep acting like this is just normal paperwork, missed payments, bank letters, legal notices — like I’m supposed to look at those papers and accept that there’s nothing else going on. But I’ve been watching this for almost two years now, and it feels connected. The bank, the loan servicer, even people at my job… it’s like they’re all moving in the same direction, quietly, carefully, to push me out of my house.\n\nSo when something happens during the day — a comment someone makes at work, the timing of a call, how certain paperwork shows up, how people suddenly get vague or avoid answering direct questions — yes, I notice it. And I do think it relates to my situation, because it fits the same pattern I’ve been seeing. That’s what people don’t understand. They look at each thing by itself and say, “That doesn’t prove anything.” But when you put it all together, it’s not random to me.\n\nAnd no amount of documents they hand me changes that. If anything, the way everyone keeps insisting it’s just procedure makes me feel like they’re trying to keep me from seeing how connected it all really is.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever considered seeking a second opinion about the situation with your house from someone you trust?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 36 / first_line_supervisor_of_retail_sales_worker / associates\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, but I know the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house, and no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’ve thought about it, yes. I mean, people keep telling me I should “get another perspective,” or “talk to someone neutral,” like if I just let one more person look at the paperwork, then all of this is going to suddenly make sense as a normal foreclosure. And I understand why that sounds reasonable from the outside. I do.

But the problem is, everyone keeps reducing it to papers. They say, “Here’s the notice, here’s the payment history, here’s the servicer letter,” as if documents explain the whole thing. That’s exactly what I keep trying to tell people — I’m here because everyone acts like this is just paperwork and missed payments, but I know it’s more than that. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together, in their own ways, to push me out of my house.

So if I go to someone for a second opinion and all they do is look at the papers, they’re going to miss the point. They’re going to say the same thing everyone else says: “This looks standard.” But I’ve been living with this for almost two years. I’ve seen how things line up, how people avoid answering directly, how the timing works, how it all keeps moving in the same direction.

I’m not against talking to someone I trust, exactly. I just don’t want to be dismissed again. Because no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing.

The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. It’s not one little misunderstanding. That’s why I’m here — because everyone keeps acting like I’m the one not understanding it, when really they’re ignoring how it all lines up.\n\nMaybe there were moments where I was exhausted and wished I could believe it was just a mistake or normal procedure, because that would be easier. No amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that other people couldn't see?\n\nPatient: Seen things that other people couldn’t see? I know the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I mean, I’ve sat there and tried to say to myself, “Okay, what if this really is just the foreclosure process, what if it’s just missed payments and paperwork and nobody is coordinating anything?” I have tried to look at it that way because everyone acts like that’s the reasonable answer.\n\nBut when I go back over everything, it doesn’t hold. It doesn’t explain why it has felt so coordinated for almost two years. Almost two years of it being in our house, in our conversations, in the mail, on the phone — just constantly there. And during that time, the way things lined up made it harder and harder for me to believe it was separate people doing separate things.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether there was some stretch in the middle where I thought, “No, they’re not connected,” I’d say no. Once it started with the foreclosure notices and those calls, I haven’t really been able to see it as anything else.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you ever changed or wavered since this all started?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say it has really wavered. I mean, I’ve gotten tired, and there have been moments where I’ve thought, maybe if I just look at the papers again, maybe there’s something I’m missing, because everyone keeps pushing that on me — like it’s just foreclosure paperwork and missed payments and normal procedure. But every time I try to look at it that way, it doesn’t fit with everything else I’ve seen.\n\nThat’s why I’m here, honestly. Because people keep acting like I’m supposed to accept whatever the bank or the loan servicer prints out and sends me, like that ends the discussion. But I know what this has been. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. It’s not one little misunderstanding. It’s been going on almost two years, and the way it all lines up is not something I can just unsee.\n\nSo yes, maybe I’ve questioned details here and there, like who knew what first or how they were communicating, but the main thing has not changed. No amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time, even for a brief moment, where you didn't believe the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job were working together against you?\n\nPatient: No. I mean, I understand why you’re asking it that way, like maybe there was some little window where I thought, “Oh, maybe this is all just ordinary paperwork.” But no, not really. Not once I saw how it was actually happening.\n\nPeople keep trying to reduce it to missed payments and foreclosure forms, like if the bank sends enough papers with logos and dates on them, then I’m supposed to just accept that as the whole truth. But that doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. That’s why I’m here — because everyone keeps acting like I’m the one not understanding it, when really they’re ignoring how it all lines up.\n\nMaybe there were moments where I was exhausted and wished I could believe it was just a mistake or normal procedure, because that would be easier. But believing it? No. No amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people couldn't hear?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I hear things everybody hears — the phone ringing, people talking outside, noises in the house, that kind of thing. Sometimes I’m on edge and I’ll notice sounds more, or I’ll think, “What was that?” and check, but I wouldn’t say I hear voices that other people can’t hear.\n\nIf you mean like a voice talking to me when nobody’s there, no. That’s not what this is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you are based on facts that most people would agree on?\n\nPatient: I think they’re based on facts, yes. I mean, maybe “most people” is where it gets complicated, because most people aren’t seeing the whole thing. They see one piece of paper from the bank, or one notice from the loan servicer, and they say, “Well, that’s just foreclosure paperwork,” or, “That’s what happens with missed payments.” But they’re not looking at how it all fits together, and they’re definitely not living inside it every day.\n\nThat’s part of why I’m here, honestly. Everyone keeps acting like if the bank prints something out, then that’s the final answer and I’m supposed to stop asking questions. But I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. It’s not just one form or one phone call or one misunderstanding. It’s been almost two years of things lining up in a way that doesn’t feel accidental.\n\nSo would most people agree? Maybe not right away, because they haven’t seen all of it the way I have. But that doesn’t mean it isn’t factual. No amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment where you completely stopped believing the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job were working together against you?\n\nPatient: No. I know it probably sounds repetitive, because I keep saying it, but no — I haven’t completely stopped believing it. I’ve had moments where I was tired, or scared, or I wished I could just accept what everyone else keeps saying, like, “It’s foreclosure paperwork, it’s missed payments, it’s just the normal process.” But that’s not the same as actually believing that.\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like this is just paperwork, like if the bank or the loan servicer sends enough notices then I’m supposed to ignore everything else. But I know what I’ve been seeing. It’s not just one letter or one phone call. It’s the way the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.\n\nSo, completely stopped believing it? No. I don’t think I have.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't think about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working against you for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: Several days in a row? No, not really. I mean, maybe there are a few hours where I’m busy at work or I’m trying to get through a shift and deal with customers and employees and all of that, and I’m not actively saying it in my head every second. But it’s still there. It doesn’t just disappear for days.\n\nBecause this isn’t some small thing I can put in a drawer. It’s my house. It’s been going on for almost two years, and everyone keeps trying to make it sound like it’s just foreclosure paperwork, missed payments, notices, normal bank procedure — but that’s not what I’m seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out. So even if I’m not thinking about it constantly every minute, something always brings it back. A letter, a call, something someone says at work, or just the thought of what they’re trying to do.\n\nSo no, I don’t think I’ve gone several days in a row without thinking about it. I wish I could, honestly, because it’s exhausting. But no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment of doubt about your beliefs regarding the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you?\n\nPatient: I mean… doubt in the way people want me to doubt it? No, not really. I’ve had moments where I get worn down, where I think, okay, maybe I should just stop fighting everyone and stop trying to explain it, because every time I bring it up people act like I’m making connections that aren’t there. But that’s not the same as doubting what I’ve seen.\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like it’s just forms and notices and I’m supposed to accept that explanation. But I know the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. That’s what keeps getting ignored. It isn’t one mistake or one late notice. It’s been going on for almost two years, and when I try to point out the pattern, people just show me more paperwork like that should settle it.\n\nSo yes, I’ve had moments where I wish I was wrong. I’ve had moments where I’m exhausted and I think, “Maybe it would be easier if this was just normal foreclosure stuff.” But then something else happens, or I think back over all of it, and it’s right there again. No amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that other people couldn't see?\n\nPatient: Seen things that other people couldn’t see? I don’t think so. I mean, not like… seeing a person standing there when nobody else does, or seeing things move that aren’t moving. That’s not really what this has been like for me.\n\nSometimes I notice things other people say they don’t notice, like the timing of things or the way certain things line up, but that’s different from seeing something with my eyes that nobody else can see. If you mean literally seeing things, like visions or objects or people that aren’t there, then no, I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nI may be more alert to things than other people, and maybe I pay attention to details they ignore, but I wouldn’t say I’m seeing things that aren’t visible.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about the conspiracy against you might be an exaggeration or not entirely based on reality?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s an exaggeration. I understand why you’re asking, because everybody keeps trying to frame it that way — like maybe I’m taking normal foreclosure notices and reading too much into them, or maybe I’m upset about missed payments and making it bigger than it is. But that’s not what this feels like from my side.\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like if they show me enough documents, then I’m supposed to say, “Oh, okay, I guess that explains everything.” But it doesn’t. I know the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. That sounds extreme when I say it out loud, I know it does, but I’ve been watching this for almost two years. It’s not one letter or one phone call or one misunderstanding.\n\nAnd no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing. If anything, the way they keep hiding behind paperwork makes me feel more like they know exactly what they’re doing. So no, I don’t think it’s not based in reality. I think other people don’t want to look at the whole pattern.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you might be incorrect?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it in the sense that people keep asking me to consider it. I mean, I’ve sat there and tried to say to myself, “Okay, what if this really is just the foreclosure process, what if it’s just missed payments and paperwork and nobody is coordinating anything?” I have tried to look at it that way because everyone acts like that’s the reasonable answer.\n\nBut when I go back over everything, it doesn’t hold. It doesn’t explain why it has felt so coordinated for almost two years. It doesn’t explain why the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job all seem to be moving in the same direction, like they’re quietly helping push me out of my house. If anything, the way everyone keeps insisting it’s just procedure makes me feel like they’re trying to keep me from seeing how connected it all really is.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered seeking a second opinion about the situation with your house from someone you trust?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about it, yes. I don’t think so. I mean, not in the way you’re asking, I don’t think.

I’ve never had something like… seeing a person standing there and then realizing nobody was there, or hearing a voice talking to me when there wasn’t anyone around. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because people keep telling me this foreclosure situation is just, you know, paperwork and missed payments and all that, but that’s not what’s really happening. I know how it sounds, but the bank, the loan servicer, and honestly even some people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house.\n\nAnd it’s been going on for almost two years, so it’s not like I’m reacting to one letter or one phone call. There have been too many things lining up, too many little changes in how people act, what gets said, what doesn’t get said. They can show me whatever documents they want, and everyone keeps acting like I’m supposed to just accept those papers as the truth, but papers can be made to look a certain way. What I’ve been seeing is something else.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job are actually working together against you?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, I don’t see how else to explain it at this point. Everyone keeps saying it’s just foreclosure paperwork or missed payments or some mistake in the servicing, but that doesn’t fit with what I’ve been seeing.\n\nThe bank says one thing, the loan servicer sends something that looks official, and then people at my job start acting differently around the same time, like they know something or they’ve been told to watch me. It’s not one single thing I can point to and say, “There, that proves it,” but when it goes on for almost two years and the same pattern keeps happening, it’s hard for me to believe it’s all accidental.\n\nSo yes, I believe they’re working together quietly to push me out of my house. And no, showing me more papers doesn’t change that, because papers can be arranged to say whatever they need them to say. What matters is what’s actually been happening around me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else believes that the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job are working together against you?\n\nPatient: I think some people understand it, or at least they act like they know more than they’re saying. But if you mean, do people come right out and say, “Yes, the bank and the loan servicer and your job are all working together,” no, most people don’t say it like that. They back away from it. They tell me I’m “stressed” or that I should just look at the paperwork, like that answers everything.\n\nBut that’s exactly the problem. Everyone keeps trying to make it sound like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like I’m supposed to ignore what I’ve been seeing for almost two years. The bank sends one thing, the loan servicer sends another, and then people at work start acting strange around the same time. Maybe they don’t all admit it out loud, but I can tell there’s more going on.\n\nSo no, I don’t have a group of people saying they believe me. But I don’t need everyone to agree with me to know what’s happening. Papers can be made to look official. That doesn’t change the way all these things have lined up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that things you see or hear on TV, radio, or in newspapers are specifically about you or sent to you?\n\nPatient: No, not really in that way. I mean, I don’t sit there watching TV thinking the anchor is talking directly to me or that the newspaper is sending me some hidden message, if that’s what you mean.\n\nWhat I’m talking about is more concrete than that. It’s the foreclosure, the bank, the loan servicer, and the way people at my job started acting around it. Everyone keeps trying to turn it into, “Oh, it’s just paperwork, it’s missed payments, it’s normal procedure,” but that doesn’t explain what I’ve been seeing over and over for almost two years. The papers they send don’t settle it for me, because papers can be arranged to look official.\n\nSo, no, I wouldn’t say TV or radio or newspapers are specifically about me. It’s more that the people directly involved in my house and my job are connected in ways they won’t admit.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't believe the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job were working together against you?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, at the very beginning, before it all became so obvious, I tried to tell myself it was just normal bank problems or some mix-up with the loan servicer. But that didn’t last, because once the foreclosure notices started coming and the bank calls started taking over our home life, it was like everything changed.\n\nThat’s been going on for about a year and nine months now. Almost two years of it being in our house, in our conversations, in the mail, on the phone — just constantly there. And during that time, the way things lined up made it harder and harder for me to believe it was separate people doing separate things.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether there was some stretch in the middle where I thought, “No, they’re not connected,” I’d say no. Once it started with the foreclosure notices and those calls, I haven’t really been able to see it as anything else.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you ever changed or wavered since this all started?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say it has really wavered. I mean, I’ve gotten tired, and there have been moments where I’ve thought, maybe if I just look at the papers again, maybe there’s something I’m missing, because everyone keeps pushing that on me — like it’s just foreclosure paperwork and missed payments and normal procedure. But every time I try to look at it that way, it doesn’t fit with everything else I’ve seen.\n\nThat’s why I’m here, honestly. Because people keep acting like I’m supposed to accept whatever the bank or the loan servicer prints out and sends me, like that ends the discussion. But I know what this has been. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. It’s not one little misunderstanding. It’s been going on almost two years, and the way it all lines up is not something I can just unsee.\n\nSo yes, maybe I’ve questioned details here and there, like who knew what first or how they were communicating, but the main thing has not changed. No amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time, even for a brief moment, where you didn't believe the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job were working together against you?\n\nPatient: No. I mean, I understand why you’re asking it that way, like maybe there was some little window where I thought, “Oh, maybe this is all just ordinary paperwork.” But no, not really. Not once I saw how it was actually happening.\n\nPeople keep trying to reduce it to missed payments and foreclosure forms, like if the bank sends enough papers with logos and dates on them, then I’m supposed to just accept that as the whole truth. But that doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. That’s why I’m here — because everyone keeps acting like I’m the one not understanding it, when really they’re ignoring how it all lines up.\n\nMaybe there were moments where I was exhausted and wished I could believe it was just a mistake or normal procedure, because that would be easier. But believing it? No. No amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people couldn't hear?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I hear things everybody hears — the phone ringing, people talking outside, noises in the house, that kind of thing. Sometimes I’m on edge and I’ll notice sounds more, or I’ll think, “What was that?” and check, but I wouldn’t say I hear voices that other people can’t hear.\n\nIf you mean like a voice talking to me when nobody’s there, no. That’s not what this is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you are based on facts that most people would agree on?\n\nPatient: I think they’re based on facts, yes. I mean, maybe “most people” is where it gets complicated, because most people aren’t seeing the whole thing. They see one piece of paper from the bank, or one notice from the loan servicer, and they say, “Well, that’s just foreclosure paperwork,” or, “That’s what happens with missed payments.” But they’re not looking at how it all fits together, and they’re definitely not living inside it every day.\n\nThat’s part of why I’m here, honestly. Everyone keeps acting like if the bank prints something out, then that’s the final answer and I’m supposed to stop asking questions. But I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. It’s not just one form or one phone call or one misunderstanding. It’s been almost two years of things lining up in a way that doesn’t feel accidental.\n\nSo would most people agree? Maybe not right away, because they haven’t seen all of it the way I have. But that doesn’t mean it isn’t factual. No amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment where you completely stopped believing the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job were working together against you?\n\nPatient: No. I know it probably sounds repetitive, because I keep saying it, but no — I haven’t completely stopped believing it. I’ve had moments where I was tired, or scared, or I wished I could just accept what everyone else keeps saying, like, “It’s foreclosure paperwork, it’s missed payments, it’s just the normal process.” But that’s not the same as actually believing that.\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like this is just paperwork, like if the bank or the loan servicer sends enough notices then I’m supposed to ignore everything else. But I know what I’ve been seeing. It’s not just one letter or one phone call. It’s the way the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.\n\nSo, completely stopped believing it? No. I don’t think I have.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't think about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working against you for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: Several days in a row? No, not really. I mean, maybe there are a few hours where I’m busy at work or I’m trying to get through a shift and deal with customers and employees and all of that, and I’m not actively saying it in my head every second. But it’s still there. It doesn’t just disappear for days.\n\nBecause this isn’t some small thing I can put in a drawer. It’s my house. It’s been going on for almost two years, and everyone keeps trying to make it sound like it’s just foreclosure paperwork, missed payments, notices, normal bank procedure — but that’s not what I’m seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out. So even if I’m not thinking about it constantly every minute, something always brings it back. A letter, a call, something someone says at work, or just the thought of what they’re trying to do.\n\nSo no, I don’t think I’ve gone several days in a row without thinking about it. I wish I could, honestly, because it’s exhausting. But no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment of doubt about your beliefs regarding the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you?\n\nPatient: I mean… doubt in the way people want me to doubt it? No, not really. I’ve had moments where I get worn down, where I think, okay, maybe I should just stop fighting everyone and stop trying to explain it, because every time I bring it up people act like I’m making connections that aren’t there. But that’s not the same as doubting what I’ve seen.\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like it’s just forms and notices and I’m supposed to accept that explanation. But I know the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. That’s what keeps getting ignored. It isn’t one mistake or one late notice. It’s been going on for almost two years, and when I try to point out the pattern, people just show me more paperwork like that should settle it.\n\nSo yes, I’ve had moments where I wish I was wrong. I’ve had moments where I’m exhausted and I think, “Maybe it would be easier if this was just normal foreclosure stuff.” But then something else happens, or I think back over all of it, and it’s right there again. No amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that other people couldn't see?\n\nPatient: Seen things that other people couldn’t see? I don’t think so. I mean, not like… seeing a person standing there when nobody else does, or seeing things move that aren’t moving. That’s not really what this has been like for me.\n\nSometimes I notice things other people say they don’t notice, like the timing of things or the way certain things line up, but that’s different from seeing something with my eyes that nobody else can see. If you mean literally seeing things, like visions or objects or people that aren’t there, then no, I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nI may be more alert to things than other people, and maybe I pay attention to details they ignore, but I wouldn’t say I’m seeing things that aren’t visible.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about the conspiracy against you might be an exaggeration or not entirely based on reality?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s an exaggeration. I understand why you’re asking, because everybody keeps trying to frame it that way — like maybe I’m taking normal foreclosure notices and reading too much into them, or maybe I’m upset about missed payments and making it bigger than it is. But that’s not what this feels like from my side.\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like if they show me enough documents, then I’m supposed to say, “Oh, okay, I guess that explains everything.” But it doesn’t. I know the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. That sounds extreme when I say it out loud, I know it does, but I’ve been watching this for almost two years. It’s not one letter or one phone call or one misunderstanding.\n\nAnd no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing. If anything, the way they keep hiding behind paperwork makes me feel more like they know exactly what they’re doing. So no, I don’t think it’s not based in reality. I think other people don’t want to look at the whole pattern.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you might be incorrect?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it in the sense that people keep asking me to consider it. I mean, I’ve sat there and tried to say to myself, “Okay, what if this really is just the foreclosure process, what if it’s just missed payments and paperwork and nobody is coordinating anything?” I have tried to look at it that way because everyone acts like that’s the reasonable answer.\n\nBut when I go back over everything, it doesn’t hold. It doesn’t explain why it has felt so coordinated for almost two years. It doesn’t explain why the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job all seem to be moving in the same direction, like they’re quietly helping push me out of my house. People keep handing me documents like that’s supposed to erase the pattern, but paperwork doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing.\n\nSo yes, I’ve considered it because I’m being pressured to consider it, but I don’t believe I’m incorrect. I think other people are refusing to see what’s actually been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you think events or things you see in your daily life are specifically related to you or your situation?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… yes, I do think certain things are related to me and what’s been happening with the house. Not every single thing, I’m not saying the whole world revolves around me or anything like that, but there are too many things in my daily life that line up with the foreclosure situation for me to just ignore them.\n\nLike, people keep acting like this is just normal paperwork, missed payments, bank letters, legal notices — like I’m supposed to look at those papers and accept that there’s nothing else going on. But I’ve been watching this for almost two years now, and it feels connected. The bank, the loan servicer, even people at my job… it’s like they’re all moving in the same direction, quietly, carefully, to push me out of my house.\n\nSo when something happens during the day — a comment someone makes at work, the timing of a call, how certain paperwork shows up, how people suddenly get vague or avoid answering direct questions — yes, I notice it. And I do think it relates to my situation, because it fits the same pattern I’ve been seeing. That’s what people don’t understand. They look at each thing by itself and say, “That doesn’t prove anything.” But when you put it all together, it’s not random to me.\n\nAnd no amount of documents they hand me changes that. If anything, the way everyone keeps insisting it’s just procedure makes me feel like they’re trying to keep me from seeing how connected it all really is.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered seeking a second opinion about the situation with your house from someone you trust?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about it, yes. I mean, people keep telling me I should “get another perspective,” or “talk to someone neutral,” like if I just let one more person look at the paperwork, then all of this is going to suddenly make sense as a normal foreclosure. And I understand why that sounds reasonable from the outside. I do.\n\nBut the problem is, everyone keeps reducing it to papers. They say, “Here’s the notice, here’s the payment history, here’s the servicer letter,” as if documents explain the whole thing. That’s exactly what I keep trying to tell people — I’m here because everyone acts like this is just paperwork and missed payments, but I know it’s more than that. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together, in their own ways, to push me out of my house.\n\nSo if I go to someone for a second opinion and all they do is look at the papers, they’re going to miss the point. They’re going to say the same thing everyone else says: “This looks standard.” But I’ve been living with this for almost two years. I’ve seen how things line up, how people avoid answering directly, how the timing works, how it all keeps moving in the same direction.\n\nI’m not against talking to someone I trust, exactly. I just don’t want to be dismissed again. Because no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen or heard things that you knew were not real?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, not in the way you’re asking, I don’t think.\n\nI’ve never had something like… seeing a person standing there and then realizing nobody was there, or hearing a voice talking to me when there wasn’t anyone around. That’s not really what this has been like for me. What I’m talking about is more that things happen, and people say things, and the timing of it feels connected. But it’s not like I’m seeing imaginary things or hearing things that aren’t there.\n\nSometimes people make it sound like if I believe there’s a pattern, then I must be hallucinating or something, and that’s not it. I see real documents. I get real calls. People at work really do say things, or avoid saying things. The bank really does send notices. So no, I wouldn’t say I’ve seen or heard things I knew weren’t real. That’s not my experience.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

It’s the way the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.\n\nSo, completely stopped believing it? No. I don’t think I have.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't think about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working against you for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: Several days in a row? No, not really. I mean, maybe there are a few hours where I’m busy at work or I’m trying to get through a shift and deal with customers and employees and all of that, and I’m not actively saying it in my head every second. But it’s still there. It doesn’t just disappear for days.\n\nBecause this isn’t some small thing I can put in a drawer. It’s my house. It’s been going on for almost two years, and everyone keeps trying to make it sound like it’s just foreclosure paperwork, missed payments, notices, normal bank procedure — but that’s not what I’m seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out. So even if I’m not thinking about it constantly every minute, something always brings it back. A letter, a call, something someone says at work, or just the thought of what they’re trying to do.\n\nSo no, I don’t think I’ve gone several days in a row without thinking about it. I wish I could, honestly, because it’s exhausting. But no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment of doubt about your beliefs regarding the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you?\n\nPatient: I mean… doubt in the way people want me to doubt it? No, not really. I’ve had moments where I get worn down, where I think, okay, maybe I should just stop fighting everyone and stop trying to explain it, because every time I bring it up people act like I’m making connections that aren’t there. But that’s not the same as doubting what I’ve seen.\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like it’s just forms and notices and I’m supposed to accept that explanation. But I know the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. That’s what keeps getting ignored. It isn’t one mistake or one late notice. It’s been going on for almost two years, and when I try to point out the pattern, people just show me more paperwork like that should settle it.\n\nSo yes, I’ve had moments where I wish I was wrong. I’ve had moments where I’m exhausted and I think, “Maybe it would be easier if this was just normal foreclosure stuff.” But then something else happens, or I think back over all of it, and it’s right there again. No amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that other people couldn't see?\n\nPatient: Seen things that other people couldn’t see? I don’t think so. I mean, not like… seeing a person standing there when nobody else does, or seeing things move that aren’t moving. That’s not really what this has been like for me.\n\nSometimes I notice things other people say they don’t notice, like the timing of things or the way certain things line up, but that’s different from seeing something with my eyes that nobody else can see. If you mean literally seeing things, like visions or objects or people that aren’t there, then no, I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nI may be more alert to things than other people, and maybe I pay attention to details they ignore, but I wouldn’t say I’m seeing things that aren’t visible.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about the conspiracy against you might be an exaggeration or not entirely based on reality?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s an exaggeration. I understand why you’re asking, because everybody keeps trying to frame it that way — like maybe I’m taking normal foreclosure notices and reading too much into them, or maybe I’m upset about missed payments and making it bigger than it is. But that’s not what this feels like from my side.\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like if they show me enough documents, then I’m supposed to say, “Oh, okay, I guess that explains everything.” But it doesn’t. I know the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. That sounds extreme when I say it out loud, I know it does, but I’ve been watching this for almost two years. It’s not one letter or one phone call or one misunderstanding.\n\nAnd no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing. If anything, the way they keep hiding behind paperwork makes me feel more like they know exactly what they’re doing. So no, I don’t think it’s not based in reality. I think other people don’t want to look at the whole pattern.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you might be incorrect?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it in the sense that people keep asking me to consider it. I mean, I’ve sat there and tried to say to myself, “Okay, what if this really is just the foreclosure process, what if it’s just missed payments and paperwork and nobody is coordinating anything?” I have tried to look at it that way because everyone acts like that’s the reasonable answer.\n\nBut when I go back over everything, it doesn’t hold. It doesn’t explain why it has felt so coordinated for almost two years. It doesn’t explain why the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job all seem to be moving in the same direction, like they’re quietly helping push me out of my house. People keep handing me documents like that’s supposed to erase the pattern, but paperwork doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing.\n\nSo yes, I’ve considered it because I’m being pressured to consider it, but I don’t believe I’m incorrect. I think other people are refusing to see what’s actually been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you think events or things you see in your daily life are specifically related to you or your situation?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… yes, I do think certain things are related to me and what’s been happening with the house. Not every single thing, I’m not saying the whole world revolves around me or anything like that, but there are too many things in my daily life that line up with the foreclosure situation for me to just ignore them.\n\nLike, people keep acting like this is just normal paperwork, missed payments, bank letters, legal notices — like I’m supposed to look at those papers and accept that there’s nothing else going on. But I’ve been watching this for almost two years now, and it feels connected. The bank, the loan servicer, even people at my job… it’s like they’re all moving in the same direction, quietly, carefully, to push me out of my house.\n\nSo when something happens during the day — a comment someone makes at work, the timing of a call, how certain paperwork shows up, how people suddenly get vague or avoid answering direct questions — yes, I notice it. No… I don’t think so. Not like voices talking when I’m alone, if that’s what you mean.

I mean, sometimes when the house is quiet I’ll replay conversations in my head — things someone at the bank said, or something from work, or I’ll think about the wording in a letter — but that’s my own thoughts. It’s not like I’m hearing somebody else speaking in the room or through the walls or anything like that.

And I know people ask because they’re trying to understand, but I don’t want this to get turned into something it isn’t. What I’ve been describing isn’t voices. It’s things that actually happen and then the way they fit together. So no, I haven’t heard voices when nobody else was around.

I mean, at the very beginning, before it all became so obvious, I tried to tell myself it was just normal bank problems or some mix-up with the loan servicer. But that didn’t last, because once the foreclosure notices started coming and the bank calls started taking over our home life, it was like everything changed.\n\nThat’s been going on for about a year and nine months now. Almost two years of it being in our house, in our conversations, in the mail, on the phone — just constantly there. And during that time, the way things lined up made it harder and harder for me to believe it was separate people doing separate things.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether there was some stretch in the middle where I thought, “No, they’re not connected,” I’d say no. Once it started with the foreclosure notices and those calls, I haven’t really been able to see it as anything else.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you ever changed or wavered since this all started?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say it has really wavered. I mean, I’ve gotten tired, and there have been moments where I’ve thought, maybe if I just look at the papers again, maybe there’s something I’m missing, because everyone keeps pushing that on me — like it’s just foreclosure paperwork and missed payments and normal procedure. But every time I try to look at it that way, it doesn’t fit with everything else I’ve seen.\n\nThat’s why I’m here, honestly. Because people keep acting like I’m supposed to accept whatever the bank or the loan servicer prints out and sends me, like that ends the discussion. But I know what this has been. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. It’s not one little misunderstanding. It’s been going on almost two years, and the way it all lines up is not something I can just unsee.\n\nSo yes, maybe I’ve questioned details here and there, like who knew what first or how they were communicating, but the main thing has not changed. No amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time, even for a brief moment, where you didn't believe the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job were working together against you?\n\nPatient: No. I mean, I understand why you’re asking it that way, like maybe there was some little window where I thought, “Oh, maybe this is all just ordinary paperwork.” But no, not really. Not once I saw how it was actually happening.\n\nPeople keep trying to reduce it to missed payments and foreclosure forms, like if the bank sends enough papers with logos and dates on them, then I’m supposed to just accept that as the whole truth. But that doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. That’s why I’m here — because everyone keeps acting like I’m the one not understanding it, when really they’re ignoring how it all lines up.\n\nMaybe there were moments where I was exhausted and wished I could believe it was just a mistake or normal procedure, because that would be easier. But believing it? No. No amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people couldn't hear?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I hear things everybody hears — the phone ringing, people talking outside, noises in the house, that kind of thing. Sometimes I’m on edge and I’ll notice sounds more, or I’ll think, “What was that?” and check, but I wouldn’t say I hear voices that other people can’t hear.\n\nIf you mean like a voice talking to me when nobody’s there, no. That’s not what this is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you are based on facts that most people would agree on?\n\nPatient: I think they’re based on facts, yes. I mean, maybe “most people” is where it gets complicated, because most people aren’t seeing the whole thing. They see one piece of paper from the bank, or one notice from the loan servicer, and they say, “Well, that’s just foreclosure paperwork,” or, “That’s what happens with missed payments.” But they’re not looking at how it all fits together, and they’re definitely not living inside it every day.\n\nThat’s part of why I’m here, honestly. Everyone keeps acting like if the bank prints something out, then that’s the final answer and I’m supposed to stop asking questions. But I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. It’s not just one form or one phone call or one misunderstanding. It’s been almost two years of things lining up in a way that doesn’t feel accidental.\n\nSo would most people agree? Maybe not right away, because they haven’t seen all of it the way I have. But that doesn’t mean it isn’t factual. No amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment where you completely stopped believing the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job were working together against you?\n\nPatient: No. I know it probably sounds repetitive, because I keep saying it, but no — I haven’t completely stopped believing it. I’ve had moments where I was tired, or scared, or I wished I could just accept what everyone else keeps saying, like, “It’s foreclosure paperwork, it’s missed payments, it’s just the normal process.” But that’s not the same as actually believing that.\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like this is just paperwork, like if the bank or the loan servicer sends enough notices then I’m supposed to ignore everything else. But I know what I’ve been seeing. It’s not just one letter or one phone call. It’s the way the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.\n\nSo, completely stopped believing it? No. I don’t think I have.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you didn't think about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working against you for several days in a row?\n\nPatient: Several days in a row? No, not really. I mean, maybe there are a few hours where I’m busy at work or I’m trying to get through a shift and deal with customers and employees and all of that, and I’m not actively saying it in my head every second. But it’s still there. It doesn’t just disappear for days.\n\nBecause this isn’t some small thing I can put in a drawer. It’s my house. It’s been going on for almost two years, and everyone keeps trying to make it sound like it’s just foreclosure paperwork, missed payments, notices, normal bank procedure — but that’s not what I’m seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out. So even if I’m not thinking about it constantly every minute, something always brings it back. A letter, a call, something someone says at work, or just the thought of what they’re trying to do.\n\nSo no, I don’t think I’ve gone several days in a row without thinking about it. I wish I could, honestly, because it’s exhausting. But no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment of doubt about your beliefs regarding the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you?\n\nPatient: I mean… doubt in the way people want me to doubt it? No, not really. I’ve had moments where I get worn down, where I think, okay, maybe I should just stop fighting everyone and stop trying to explain it, because every time I bring it up people act like I’m making connections that aren’t there. But that’s not the same as doubting what I’ve seen.\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like it’s just forms and notices and I’m supposed to accept that explanation. But I know the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. That’s what keeps getting ignored. It isn’t one mistake or one late notice. It’s been going on for almost two years, and when I try to point out the pattern, people just show me more paperwork like that should settle it.\n\nSo yes, I’ve had moments where I wish I was wrong. I’ve had moments where I’m exhausted and I think, “Maybe it would be easier if this was just normal foreclosure stuff.” But then something else happens, or I think back over all of it, and it’s right there again. No amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that other people couldn't see?\n\nPatient: Seen things that other people couldn’t see? I don’t think so. I mean, not like… seeing a person standing there when nobody else does, or seeing things move that aren’t moving. That’s not really what this has been like for me.\n\nSometimes I notice things other people say they don’t notice, like the timing of things or the way certain things line up, but that’s different from seeing something with my eyes that nobody else can see. If you mean literally seeing things, like visions or objects or people that aren’t there, then no, I haven’t noticed anything like that.\n\nI may be more alert to things than other people, and maybe I pay attention to details they ignore, but I wouldn’t say I’m seeing things that aren’t visible.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about the conspiracy against you might be an exaggeration or not entirely based on reality?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s an exaggeration. I understand why you’re asking, because everybody keeps trying to frame it that way — like maybe I’m taking normal foreclosure notices and reading too much into them, or maybe I’m upset about missed payments and making it bigger than it is. But that’s not what this feels like from my side.\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just paperwork and missed payments, like if they show me enough documents, then I’m supposed to say, “Oh, okay, I guess that explains everything.” But it doesn’t. I know the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. That sounds extreme when I say it out loud, I know it does, but I’ve been watching this for almost two years. It’s not one letter or one phone call or one misunderstanding.\n\nAnd no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing. If anything, the way they keep hiding behind paperwork makes me feel more like they know exactly what they’re doing. So no, I don’t think it’s not based in reality. I think other people don’t want to look at the whole pattern.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you might be incorrect?\n\nPatient: I’ve considered it in the sense that people keep asking me to consider it. I mean, I’ve sat there and tried to say to myself, “Okay, what if this really is just the foreclosure process, what if it’s just missed payments and paperwork and nobody is coordinating anything?” I have tried to look at it that way because everyone acts like that’s the reasonable answer.\n\nBut when I go back over everything, it doesn’t hold. It doesn’t explain why it has felt so coordinated for almost two years. It doesn’t explain why the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job all seem to be moving in the same direction, like they’re quietly helping push me out of my house. People keep handing me documents like that’s supposed to erase the pattern, but paperwork doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing.\n\nSo yes, I’ve considered it because I’m being pressured to consider it, but I don’t believe I’m incorrect. I think other people are refusing to see what’s actually been happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you think events or things you see in your daily life are specifically related to you or your situation?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… yes, I do think certain things are related to me and what’s been happening with the house. Not every single thing, I’m not saying the whole world revolves around me or anything like that, but there are too many things in my daily life that line up with the foreclosure situation for me to just ignore them.\n\nLike, people keep acting like this is just normal paperwork, missed payments, bank letters, legal notices — like I’m supposed to look at those papers and accept that there’s nothing else going on. But I’ve been watching this for almost two years now, and it feels connected. The bank, the loan servicer, even people at my job… it’s like they’re all moving in the same direction, quietly, carefully, to push me out of my house.\n\nSo when something happens during the day — a comment someone makes at work, the timing of a call, how certain paperwork shows up, how people suddenly get vague or avoid answering direct questions — yes, I notice it. And I do think it relates to my situation, because it fits the same pattern I’ve been seeing. That’s what people don’t understand. They look at each thing by itself and say, “That doesn’t prove anything.” But when you put it all together, it’s not random to me.\n\nAnd no amount of documents they hand me changes that. If anything, the way everyone keeps insisting it’s just procedure makes me feel like they’re trying to keep me from seeing how connected it all really is.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered seeking a second opinion about the situation with your house from someone you trust?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about it, yes. I mean, people keep telling me I should “get another perspective,” or “talk to someone neutral,” like if I just let one more person look at the paperwork, then all of this is going to suddenly make sense as a normal foreclosure. And I understand why that sounds reasonable from the outside. I do.\n\nBut the problem is, everyone keeps reducing it to papers. They say, “Here’s the notice, here’s the payment history, here’s the servicer letter,” as if documents explain the whole thing. That’s exactly what I keep trying to tell people — I’m here because everyone acts like this is just paperwork and missed payments, but I know it’s more than that. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together, in their own ways, to push me out of my house.\n\nSo if I go to someone for a second opinion and all they do is look at the papers, they’re going to miss the point. They’re going to say the same thing everyone else says: “This looks standard.” But I’ve been living with this for almost two years. I’ve seen how things line up, how people avoid answering directly, how the timing works, how it all keeps moving in the same direction.\n\nI’m not against talking to someone I trust, exactly. I just don’t want to be dismissed again. Because no amount of papers they show me changes what I’ve been seeing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen or heard things that you knew were not real?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, not in the way you’re asking, I don’t think.\n\nI’ve never had something like… seeing a person standing there and then realizing nobody was there, or hearing a voice talking to me when there wasn’t anyone around. That’s not really what this has been like for me. What I’m talking about is more that things happen, and people say things, and the timing of it feels connected. So no, I wouldn’t say I’ve seen or heard things I knew weren’t real. That’s not my experience.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices when nobody else was around?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. It’s not like I’m hearing somebody else speaking in the room or through the walls or anything like that.\n\nAnd I know people ask because they’re trying to understand, but I don’t want this to get turned into something it isn’t. What I’ve been describing isn’t voices. It’s things that actually happen and then the way they fit together. So no, I haven’t heard voices when nobody else was around.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
